In the [2021 Census of Population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census) conducted by [Statistics Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_Canada), Ottawa had a population of 1,017,449 living in 407,252 of its 427,113 total private dwellings, a change of 8.9% from its 2016 population of 934,243. With a land area of 2,788.2 square kilometres (1,076.5 sq mi), it had a population density of 364.9/km<sup>2</sup> (945.1/sq mi) in 2021.[^cite_note-2021census-265]

As of 2021, the Ottawa-Gatineau [census metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_metropolitan_area) (CMA) had a population of 1,488,307 living in 604,721 of its 638,013 total private dwellings, a change of 8.5% from its 2016 population of 1,371,576. With a land area of 8,046.99 km<sup>2</sup> (3,106.96 sq mi), it had a population density of 185.0/km<sup>2</sup> (479.0/sq mi) in 2021.[^cite_note-2021censusCMA-266]

Ottawa's median age of 40.0 is slightly below the provincial (41.6) and national (41.6) averages as of 2021.[^cite_note-\:29-267][^cite_note-268] Youths under 15 constituted 16.4% of the city's total population in 2021, while those of retirement age (65 years and older) made up 16.9%.[^cite_note-\:29-267]

### Race, immigrant status and ethnicity

As of 2021, approximately 64.9% of Ottawa's population were white or European, while 2.6% were [Indigenous](196075.md), and 32.5% were visible minorities (higher than the national percentage of 26.5%).[^cite_note-269]

The [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census) reported that [immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Canada) (individuals born outside Canada) number 259,215, 25.9% of the population of Ottawa. Of this immigrant population, the top countries of origin were [China](5405.md) (20,320 persons or 7.8%), [India](14533.md) (16,200 persons or 6.2%), [United Kingdom](31717.md) (14,760 persons or 5.7%), [Lebanon](17771.md) (11,900 persons or 4.6%), [Philippines](23440.md) (10,505 persons or 4.1%), [United States of America](3434750.md) (8,795 persons or 3.4%), [Haiti](13373.md) (6,710 persons or 2.6%), [Syria](7515849.md) (6,370 persons or 2.5%), [Vietnam](202354.md) (6,155 persons or 2.4%), and [Iran](14653.md) (6,000 persons or 2.3%).[^cite_note-2021censusB-270]

### Religion

According to the [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census), religious groups in Ottawa included:[^cite_note-2021censusB-270]

- [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Canada) (528,700 persons or 52.8%)

- [Irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Canada) (316,740 persons or 31.6%)

- [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Canada) (98,920 persons or 9.9%)

- [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Canada) (20,300 persons or 2.0%)

- [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Canada) (10,800 persons or 1.1%)

- [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_in_Canada) (10,600 persons or 1.1%)

- [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism_in_Canada) (6,375 persons or 0.6%)

- [Indigenous Spirituality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mythologies_of_the_indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) (445 persons or <0.1%)

- Other (8,055 persons or 0.8%)

Of the Christian population, Catholicism was the most widely declared with 303,820 adherents, with the next largest specified group being Anglican with 41,175 adherents.

### Language

As of 2021, those who identify their mother tongue as [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) constitute 58.2 percent, while those with [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language) as their mother tongue make up 12.5 percent of the population.[^cite_note-271] Regarding respondents' knowledge of one or both official languages, 60.6 percent and 1.4 percent of the population know English and French only, respectively, while 36.4 percent know both official languages.[^cite_note-272] [Bilingualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilingualism_in_Ottawa) became official policy for the conduct of municipal business in 2002,[^cite_note-273] making it the largest city in Canada with both English and French as co-official languages, as Vancouver, Montreal and Toronto do not have official bilingualism policies. An additional 23.1 percent of the population list languages other than English and French as their mother tongue. These include [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language) (4.3%), [Mandarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese) (2.2%), [Spanish](26825.md) (1.4%), [Italian](14708.md) (0.8%), and many others.[^cite_note-274]

The overall Ottawa–Gatineau census metropolitan area (CMA) has a larger proportion of French speakers than Ottawa since [Gatineau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatineau)'s population's first language is mostly French.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276]

## Economy

See also: [Economy of Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Ontario)

As of 2016, the region of Ottawa-Gatineau has the sixth-highest total household income of all Canadian metropolitan areas (\$82,053), and the Ontario portion more directly overlapping the City of Ottawa has a higher household income (\$86,451).[^cite_note-277] The median household income after taxes in the City of Ottawa is \$88,000 in 2021 was higher than the national median of \$73,000.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] Prior to the COVID pandemic, Ottawa's unemployment rate has remained below the national and provincial unemployment rates since 2006, with a rate of 5.2% in April 2022, low compared to the decade preceding.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] Ottawa is ranked the best city in North America for quality of life by Numbeo, but falls short of other Canadian cities in other rankings such as Vancouver and Toronto in Mercer's rankings.[^cite_note-Quality2026-282][^cite_note-283]

Ottawa's primary employer is the [Public Service of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Service_of_Canada).[^cite_note-\:9-284] The federal government is the city's largest employer, employing over 128,000 individuals from the National Capital Region as of 2016.[^cite_note-\:9-284] The national headquarters for many federal departments are in Ottawa and Gatineau, particularly throughout Centretown, [Tunneys Pasture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunney's_Pasture), and in the [Terrasses de la Chaudière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrasses_de_la_Chaudi%C3%A8re) and [Place du Portage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_du_Portage) complexes in Hull. The [National Defence Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Defence_Headquarters_\(Canada\)) in Ottawa is the main command centre for the [Canadian Armed Forces](182792.md). It hosts the [Department of National Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_National_Defence_\(Canada\)).[^cite_note-285]

As Canada's national capital, tourism is an important part of Ottawa's economy, particularly after the [150th anniversary of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/150th_anniversary_of_Canada), centred in Ottawa. The lead-up to the festivities saw much investment in civic infrastructure, upgrades to tourist infrastructure and increases in national cultural attractions.[^cite_note-286] Depending on methodology and source, the National Capital Region annually attracts an estimated 9.8 to 11 million tourists, who spend about 2.6 billion dollars and support approximately 23,000 jobs directly.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-citizennews-28]



[Kanata Research Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanata_Research_Park) is Canada's largest technology park.

In addition to the economic activities that come with being the national capital, Ottawa is an important technology centre; in 2015, its 1800 companies employed approximately 63,400 people.[^cite_note-290] The concentration of companies in this industry earned the city the nickname of "Silicon Valley North."[^cite_note-Shavinina2004-130] Large technology companies such as Nortel, Corel, [Mitel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitel), [Cognos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognos), [Shopify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shopify) and [JDS Uniphase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JDS_Uniphase) were founded in the city.[^cite_note-TreviñoDoutriaux2006j-291][^cite_note-292] As of 2007, Ottawa also had regional offices for [Nokia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nokia), [Adobe Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adobe_Systems), [Bell Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Canada) and [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM).[^cite_note-NovakowskiTremblay2007x-293] Many tech companies, including Mitel, are in the western part of the city (formerly Kanata).[^cite_note-294] The "tech sector" was doing particularly well prior to the dot-com burst, but remains generally strong as of 2016, with more technology jobs than at any time prior to the new millennium.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-Demographics2002-296][^cite_note-\:9-284] As of 2011, [Nordion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordion), [Abbott Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbott_Laboratories) and the National Research Council of Canada and [OHRI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHRI) are part of the life science sector in Ottawa.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]

The health sector is another major employer, which employs over 64,000 people in the city as of the 2020 census.[^cite_note-\:3-18] The City of Ottawa is the second largest employer as of 2010,[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-OttawaCompensation-300] with approximately 2,100 people employed by the Ottawa Police service as of 2022, and 13,300 full-time equivalent non-police employees as of 2019.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302]

All major clusters tracked by the city saw increases in employment between 2014 and 2019, with the exception of culture and tourism.[^cite_note-\:11-303] Major areas of growth in the 2010s included local and federal administration, finance and accommodation.[^cite_note-\:9-284] Between 2008 and 2020, there was growth in the number of government employees and a reduction in high-tech jobs, a reversal of previous trends from 2003 to 2008.[^cite_note-\:10-304][^cite_note-\:11-303] The city has experienced a weak labour market recovery in the years since the onset of the COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-305] Ottawa has also seen above average disruption to the economy due to American tariff policy during the second term of President Donald Trump.[^cite_note-306]

Ottawa already has the largest rural economy among Canada's major cities.[^cite_note-\:2-307] In Ottawa, the rural economy contributes over \$1 billion to the GDP. Agriculture alone accounts for \$400 million, \$136.7 million of which is farm-gate sales.[^cite_note-\:2-307] Rural economic activity includes agriculture, retail sales, construction, forestry and mining (specifically of [aggregates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aggregate_\(composite\))), tourism, manufacturing, personal and business services, and transportation.[^cite_note-\:2-307] Rural employment expanded by 18% from 1996 to 2001.[^cite_note-\:2-307]

### Media

Further information: [Media in Ottawa–Gatineau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Ottawa%E2%80%93Gatineau)

Two daily local newspapers are printed in Ottawa: two English newspapers, the *[Ottawa Citizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Citizen)* established as *the Bytown Packet* in 1845 and the *[Ottawa Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Sun).[^cite_note-308]* The city's last French language daily, *[Le Droit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Droit),* has become an online only paper after a 2020-2023 run as a weekly.[^cite_note-309] The city is also home to local stations of the television broadcast networks and systems [CBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBC_News) and [CTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTV_Television_Network), as well as English and French radio stations.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313]

In addition to the market's local media services, Ottawa is home to several national media operations, including [CPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPAC_\(TV_channel\)) (a private-sector broadcaster of government proceedings)[^cite_note-314] and the parliamentary bureau staff of various major newsgathering organizations in television, radio and print.[^cite_note-315] The city is also home to the head office of the [Canadian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Broadcasting_Corporation).[^cite_note-316]

## Education

Further information: [List of schools in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Ottawa)



Established in 1848, the [University of Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ottawa) is the oldest post-secondary institution in the city.



[Carleton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carleton_University) in 2022



[Saint Paul University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul_University)

### Primary and secondary education

As of 2025, Ottawa has four main public school boards: English, English-Catholic, French, and French-Catholic. The English-language [Ottawa-Carleton District School Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa-Carleton_District_School_Board) (OCDSB) is the largest board with 116 primary schools, 26 secondary schools, 5 alternative learning sites and 1 adult education school,[^cite_note-317] followed by the English-Catholic [Ottawa Catholic School Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Catholic_School_Board) with 90 schools.[^cite_note-318] The two French-language boards are the French-Catholic *[Conseil des écoles catholiques du Centre-Est](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_des_%C3%A9coles_catholiques_du_Centre-Est)* with 46 primary schools and 11 secondary schools,[^cite_note-319] and the French *[Conseil des écoles publiques de l'Est de l'Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_des_%C3%A9coles_publiques_de_l'Est_de_l'Ontario)* with 44 schools.[^cite_note-320] Ottawa also has numerous [private schools](612857.md) which are not part of a board.[^cite_note-321]

The [Ottawa Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Public_Library) was created in 1906 as part of the [Carnegie library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_library) system.[^cite_note-322] As of 2026 the library system has 1.8 million items, down from 2.3 million in 2008, when it had 33 branches and 2 mobile libraries .[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] Approximately 11.5 million loans were conducted in 2024,[^cite_note-\:15-325] approximately 8.2 million physical loans and the remainder digital items.[^cite_note-326] In 2024, the library saw 3.5 million visits.[^cite_note-\:15-325]

### Higher education and public research

Over half the population has graduated from college and/or university.[^cite_note-327] As of 2007, Ottawa was claimed by the local business journal to have the highest per capita concentration of [engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineer), [scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientist), and residents with [PhDs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PhD) in Canada.[^cite_note-328] The city has two main public universities and two main public colleges.

- [Carleton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carleton_University) was founded in 1942 to meet the needs of returning World War II veterans and later became Ontario's first private, non-denominational college.

  [^cite_note-Creating-329]

  &#x20;Over time, Carleton transitioned into a comprehensive university that Maclean's ranks 4th out of 15 in its comprehensive category.

  [^cite_note-330]

  &#x20;The university's campus sits between [Old Ottawa South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Ottawa_South) and the [Central Experimental Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Experimental_Farm).

  [^cite_note-331]

- The [University of Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ottawa) (originally named the "College of Bytown") was the first post-secondary institution established in the city in 1848.

  [^cite_note-citot-332]

  &#x20;The university later grew to become the largest English-French bilingual university in the world.

  [^cite_note-Quick-333]

  &#x20;It is also a member of the [U15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U15_Group_of_Canadian_Research_Universities), a group of universities in Canada with high levels of research funding.

  [^cite_note-334]

  &#x20;The university's main campus is in the [Sandy Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hill,_Ottawa) neighbourhood, just adjacent to the city's downtown core.

  [^cite_note-335]

  &#x20;The University of Ottawa's has a catholic-[affiliated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affiliated_school) university college, [St. Paul University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul_University).

- [Algonquin College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquin_College) is a college of applied arts and technology founded in 1967.

  [^cite_note-336]

  &#x20;Its main campus is located in the [City View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_View,_Ottawa) neighbourhood of [College Ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_Ward). The college serves the [National Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_\(Canada\)) and the outlying areas of [Eastern Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ontario).

  [^cite_note-337]

  &#x20;The college has satellite campuses in [Pembroke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pembroke,_Ontario) and, until an impending closure in August 2026, [Perth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perth,_Ontario).

  [^cite_note-338]

  [^cite_note-339]

- [Collège La Cité](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coll%C3%A8ge_La_Cit%C3%A9) is the largest French-language college in Ontario by number of students.

  [^cite_note-340]

  &#x20;Founded in 1989,

  [^cite_note-341]

  &#x20;its campus is located off the [Aviation Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviation_Parkway_\(Ottawa\)) in the [Carson Meadows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carson_Meadows) neighbourhood.

  [^cite_note-342]

Other colleges and universities in the metropolitan area are located in the neighbouring suburb of Gatineau, including the [University of Quebec in Outaouais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_du_Qu%C3%A9bec_en_Outaouais), [Cégep de l'Outaouais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9gep_de_l'Outaouais), and [Heritage College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_College_\(Gatineau\)).[^cite_note-343]

The city is also home to many federal post-secondary associations, including those representing institutions like [Polytechnics Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polytechnics_Canada),[^cite_note-344] [Universities Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_Canada),[^cite_note-345] [Colleges and Institutes Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colleges_and_Institutes_Canada),[^cite_note-346] [Association des collèges et universités de la francophonie canadienne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_des_coll%C3%A8ges_et_universit%C3%A9s_de_la_francophonie_canadienne),[^cite_note-347] and the U15;[^cite_note-348] those representing educators, such as the [Canadian Association of University Teachers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Association_of_University_Teachers);[^cite_note-349] and those representing students, such as the [Canadian Alliance of Student Associations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Alliance_of_Student_Associations),[^cite_note-350] [Canadian Federation of Students](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Federation_of_Students)[^cite_note-351] and the National Educational Association of Disabled Students.[^cite_note-352]

Public research also occurs through government laboratories and research centres such as the [National Research Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Research_Council_Canada),[^cite_note-353] various [Health Canada](216516.md) laboratories[^cite_note-354] or the [Central Experimental Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Experimental_Farm).[^cite_note-355]

### Public health

See also: [List of hospitals in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hospitals_in_Ottawa)



The [Civic Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Civic_Hospital) is one of three main campuses of [The Ottawa Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ottawa_Hospital).

There are four active general medical hospitals organizations in the city of Ottawa in six locations: The [Queensway Carleton Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queensway_Carleton_Hospital), [The Ottawa Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ottawa_Hospital) ([Civic Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Civic_Hospital), [General Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_General_Hospital), [Riverside Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverside_Hospital_of_Ottawa)), [Montfort Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montfort_Hospital), and the [Children's Hospital of Eastern Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_Hospital_of_Eastern_Ontario).[^cite_note-356] Several specialized hospital facilities are also present, such as the [University of Ottawa Heart Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ottawa_Heart_Institute), the [Royal Ottawa Mental Health Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Ottawa_Mental_Health_Centre), and Élisabeth Bruyère Hospital.[^cite_note-357] There are also several hospitals and major medical centres in neighbouring suburban communities and commuter towns.[^cite_note-358] The [University of Ottawa Faculty of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ottawa_Faculty_of_Medicine) operates [teaching hospitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teaching_hospital) in conjunction with partners throughout the city.[^cite_note-uOttawa_Med-359]

Ottawa is the headquarters of numerous major medical organizations and institutions such as [Canadian Red Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Red_Cross),[^cite_note-360] [Canadian Blood Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Blood_Services),[^cite_note-361] [Health Canada](216516.md),[^cite_note-362] [Canadian Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Medical_Association),[^cite_note-363] [Royal College of Physicians and Surgeons of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_College_of_Physicians_and_Surgeons_of_Canada),[^cite_note-364] [Canadian Nurses Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Nurses_Association),[^cite_note-365] and the [Medical Council of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Council_of_Canada).[^cite_note-366]

## Culture and contemporary life



[ByWard Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ByWard_Market) is a retail and entertainment district in Downtown Ottawa.

Traditionally, the [ByWard Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ByWard_Market) (in Lower Town), Parliament Hill, [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Ottawa), and the [Golden Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Triangle_\(Ottawa,_Canada\)) (in Centretown – Downtown) have been focal points of the cultural scenes in Ottawa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHale201159–60-367] Modern thoroughfares such as [Wellington Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellington_Street_\(Ottawa\)), [Rideau Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rideau_Street), [Sussex Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sussex_Drive), [Elgin Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elgin_Street_\(Ottawa\)), [Bank Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Street_\(Ottawa\)), [Somerset Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_Street_\(Ottawa\)), [Preston Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_Street_\(Ottawa\)), [Wellington Street West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellington_Street_West), Richmond Road in [Westboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westboro,_Ottawa), and [Sparks Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparks_Street) are home to many boutiques, museums, theatres, galleries, landmarks and memorials in addition to eating establishments, cafes, bars and nightclubs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHale201161–68-368]

As Canada's capital, Ottawa has played host to many significant cultural events in [Canadian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_history), including the first visit of the reigning [Canadian sovereign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Canada)—[King George VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_VI), with [his consort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_royal_consorts_of_Canada), [Queen Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_The_Queen_Mother)—[to his parliament, on 19 May 1939](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1939_royal_tour_of_Canada).[^cite_note-BousfieldToffoli1989-369] [VE Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_in_Europe_Day) was marked with a large celebration on 8 May 1945,[^cite_note-Granatstein2005n-370] the first raising of [the country's new national flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Canada) took place on 15 February 1965,[^cite_note-Solski2006-371][^cite_note-372] and the [centennial of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Centennial) was celebrated on 1 July 1967.[^cite_note-373] Queen [Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II) was in Ottawa on 17 April 1982, to issue a [royal proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation) of the enactment of the [Constitution Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_Act,_1982).[^cite_note-Hayes2008-374] In 1983, the then [Prince Charles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Charles) and [Diana, Princess of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana,_Princess_of_Wales) came to Ottawa for a state dinner hosted by then Prime Minister [Pierre Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Trudeau).[^cite_note-375] In 2011, Ottawa was selected as the first city to receive [Prince William, Duke of Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William,_Duke_of_Cambridge), and [Catherine, Duchess of Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine,_Duchess_of_Cambridge) during their [tour of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_royal_tour_of_Canada).[^cite_note-2011-06-30-HCH-376]

During the summer, the city hosts the [Ceremonial Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceremonial_Guard_\(Canada\)), which performs functions such as the [Changing the Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changing_the_Guard).[^cite_note-377]

Ottawa was featured in the short story collection *[For Your Eyes Only](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/For_Your_Eyes_Only_\(short_story_collection\))*, by [Ian Fleming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Fleming).[^cite_note-378]

&#x20;Ottawa's reputation with regard to culture and contemporary life does have a mixed review, with an enduring label for the city being "The Town that Fun Forgot", dating back to the 1970s and sometimes still used in local self-critique.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381]

The National War Memorial

The [National War Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_War_Memorial_\(Canada\))

### Landmarks

Main articles: [List of National Historic Sites of Canada in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Historic_Sites_of_Canada_in_Ottawa) and [List of designated heritage properties in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_designated_heritage_properties_in_Ottawa)

There is one [World Heritage Site](44940.md) in Ottawa, the [Rideau Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rideau_Canal),[^cite_note-382] along with 27 [National Historic Sites of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Site_of_Canada) in Ottawa, including the Château Laurier, the [Parliament of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Canada), Confederation Square, the former Ottawa Teachers' College and [Laurier House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurier_House).[^cite_note-383] Additionally in 1990, Notre Dame Cathedral Basilica was designated as a National Historic Site.[^cite_note-384] Many other properties of cultural value have been designated as having "heritage elements" by the City of Ottawa under Part IV of the *[Ontario Heritage Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Heritage_Act)*.[^cite_note-385]

### Arts

See also: [List of attractions in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attractions_in_Ottawa) and [List of museums in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Ottawa)



The [Canadian Museum of Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Museum_of_Nature) is Canada's national museum of [natural history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_history) and [natural science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_science).

#### Performing and visual arts

The [Ottawa Little Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Little_Theatre), founded in 1913 as the Ottawa Drama League, is Ottawa's longest-running community theatre company.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHale201160-386] Since 1969, Ottawa has been the home of the National Arts Centre, a major performing arts venue that houses four stages and is home to the [National Arts Centre Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Arts_Centre_Orchestra), and formerly hosted the [Opera Lyra Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Lyra_Ottawa) and the [Ottawa Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Symphony_Orchestra) prior to their failure and move to Carleton University, respectively.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388]

Established in 1975, the [Great Canadian Theatre Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Canadian_Theatre_Company) specializes in the production of Canadian plays at a local level.[^cite_note-389] The cities museum landscape is notable for containing six of Canada's nine national museums, the [Canada Agriculture and Food Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Agriculture_Museum), the [Canada Aviation and Space Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Aviation_and_Space_Museum), the [Canada Science and Technology Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Science_and_Technology_Museum), [Canadian Museum of Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Museum_of_Nature), [Canadian War Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_War_Museum) and [National Gallery of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Canada).[^cite_note-390] The [National Gallery of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Canada); designed by famous architect [Moshe Safdie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Safdie), it is a permanent home to the [*Maman*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maman_\(sculpture\)) sculpture.[^cite_note-391] The [Canadian War Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_War_Museum) houses a collection comprising approximately 600,000 artifacts, photos and books and was moved to an expanded facility in 2005.[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393] The Victoria Memorial Museum Building, which houses the [Canadian Museum of Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Museum_of_Nature) commenced construction in 1905 and it opened as the National Museum of Natural Sciences in 1912, and underwent a major renovation between 2004 and 2010, leading to a centrepiece Blue Whale skeleton, and the creation of a monthly nightclub experience, *Nature Nocturne*.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396]

### Cuisine

&#x20;Ottawa is home to several regional dishes. As a city with traditional [French-Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French-Canadian_cuisine) roots, staples such as [poutine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poutine) are served throughout the city. However, many consider [shawarma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawarma) a defining or especially common Ottawa dish;[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-Official_dish-398] as the city contains more shawarma restaurants than anywhere else in Canada.[^cite_note-Shawarma_Capital-399] In 2024, Ottawa City Council declared Ottawa as the "Shawarma Capital of Canada" in a unanimous motion[^cite_note-400] [Beaver tails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fried_dough), a fried dough pastry, were first created in the Ottawa Valley in the 1970s, and are popular in the city after the creators opened two permanent storefronts in the city.[^cite_note-401] [Le Cordon Bleu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Cordon_Bleu) has a long-established culinary arts institute in the central Ottawa neighbourhood of Sandy Hill, the only Le Cordon Bleu campus in North America.[^cite_note-Le_Cordon_Bleu-402] Ottawa style pizza is characterized by a pair of Ottawa pizza podcasters as being greasy, having sweeter sauce, crispy, thick cheese, thick crust, and toppings under the cheese.[^cite_note-403] Other food writers contend Ottawa-style pizza is inextricably linked with Ottawa's Lebanese pizza tradition.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405]&#x20;



[Winterlude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winterlude) is an annual [winter festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winter_festival) held in Ottawa.

### Festivals

See also: [List of festivals in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_festivals_in_Ottawa)

Ottawa hosts a variety of annual seasonal activities—such as [Winterlude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winterlude), the largest festival in Canada,[^cite_note-406] and [Canada Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Day) celebrations on Parliament Hill and surrounding downtown area,[^cite_note-407] as well as [Bluesfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Bluesfest), [Canadian Tulip Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Tulip_Festival), [Ottawa Dragon Boat Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Dragon_Boat_Festival), [Ottawa International Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_International_Jazz_Festival), [Fringe Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Fringe_Festival), [Capital Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Pride_\(Ottawa\)), and [CityFolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CityFolk_Festival), that have grown to become some of the largest festivals of their kind in the world.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413] In 2010, Ottawa's Festival industry received the [IFEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Festivals_and_Events_Association) "World Festival and Event City Award" for the category of North American cities with a population between 500,000 and 1,000,000.[^cite_note-414]

## Sports

Further information: [Sport in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Ottawa) and [List of Ottawa parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ottawa_parks)



[TD Place Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TD_Place_Stadium) is home to the [CFL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Football_League)'s [Ottawa Redblacks](16627032.md), the [CPL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Premier_League)'s [Atlético Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Ottawa), and the [NSL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Super_League)'s [Ottawa Rapid FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Rapid_FC).

### Professional sports

[Sport in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Ottawa) has a history dating back to the 19th century. The city is currently home to six professional sports teams. The [Ottawa Senators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Senators) are a professional ice hockey team playing in the [National Hockey League](21809.md).[^cite_note-415] The team shares the name with a [previous franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Senators_\(original\)) that won the [Stanley Cup](66968.md) four times between 1920 and 1927.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] The team is currently a member of the Atlantic Division and play their home games at the [Canadian Tire Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Tire_Centre).[^cite_note-WeekesBanks2002-418] In 2023, the [Ottawa Charge](74756360.md) became one of the six charter franchises of the [Professional Women's Hockey League](74699992.md) (PWHL). The Charge play home games at [TD Place Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TD_Place_Arena).[^cite_note-419]

The [Ottawa Redblacks](16627032.md) are a professional Canadian Football team playing in the [Canadian Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Football_League).[^cite_note-Ottawa_CFL-420] Formerly the [Ottawa Rough Riders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Rough_Riders) represented the city until 1996. With precursors dating back to 1876, the team adopted its identity in 1898 and was one of the oldest and longest-lived professional sports teams in North America.[^cite_note-421] The professional soccer club, [Atlético Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Ottawa), plays in the [Canadian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Premier_League). The team was founded by Spanish club [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid), and along with the Redblacks, play their home games at [TD Place Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TD_Place_Stadium).[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] [Ottawa Rapid FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Rapid_FC) of the [Northern Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Super_League) also play at TD Place Stadium. The Rapid are one of the six founding clubs in the NSL, having played in the [inaugural 2025 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Northern_Super_League_season).[^cite_note-424] The [New York Riptide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Riptide) became [Ottawa Black Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Black_Bears) when they moved to the city in 2024.[^cite_note-425] They compete in the [National Lacrosse League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Lacrosse_League) and play at the [Canadian Tire Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Tire_Centre) in [Kanata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanata,_Ontario).[^cite_note-426] The [Ottawa Blackjacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Blackjacks) are a professional basketball team, playing in the [Canadian Elite Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Elite_Basketball_League), out of the TD Place Arena.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] The [Ottawa Titans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Titans_\(baseball\)) play professional baseball in the [Frontier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_League) at [Raymond Chabot Grant Thornton Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Chabot_Grant_Thornton_Park).[^cite_note-429] Ottawa was previously home to the [Ottawa Lynx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Lynx), a [Triple-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-A_\(baseball\)) club, as well as the [Ottawa Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Champions), an independent baseball team in the [Can-Am League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can-Am_League).[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431]

### Collegiate sports

The University of Ottawa and Carleton University [varsity teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varsity_team) compete in [U Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_Sports) in various sports.[^cite_note-432] Algonquin College and Collège La Cité teams compete in the [OCAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Colleges_Athletic_Association).[^cite_note-433]

The [Carleton Ravens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carleton_Ravens) are nationally ranked in [basketball](3921.md) and soccer as of 2011.[^cite_note-434] [Carleton's men's basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carleton_Ravens_men's_basketball) program is regarded as dynastically successful, having won 17 of the last 20 [national championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W._P._McGee_Trophy).[^cite_note-Carleton_Ravens_dynasty-435] The [Ottawa Gee-Gees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Gee-Gees) are nationally ranked in basketball and soccer as of 2022.[^cite_note-436]

### Semi-professional and amateur sports

Several non-professional teams also play in Ottawa, including the [Ottawa 67's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_67's) [junior ice hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_ice_hockey) team.[^cite_note-KearneyRay2009-437]

The city is home to an assortment of amateur organized team sports such as [soccer](10568.md), [basketball](3921.md), [baseball](3850.md) and [curling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curling).[^cite_note-438] Casual recreational activities, such as [skating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_skating), [cycling](5931.md) and [skiing](28478.md) are also available.[^cite_note-Sports-439]

The city has hosted [Ottawa Race Weekend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Race_Weekend) during the 4th weekend of May since 1975.[^cite_note-440] In addition, [Ironman Triathlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ironman_Triathlon) began in Ottawa in 2025.[^cite_note-441]

## Government and politics

Further information: [List of Ottawa municipal elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ottawa_municipal_elections), [Canadian federal election results in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_federal_election_results_in_Ottawa), and [List of embassies and high commissions in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_embassies_and_high_commissions_in_Ottawa)



[Ottawa City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_City_Hall) houses the [seat of the local government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_City_Council).

The City of Ottawa is a [single-tier municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_divisions_of_Ontario#Single-tier_municipalities), meaning it is in itself a [census division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_division) and has no county or regional municipality government above it, and has no subsidiary municipalities to provide municipal services.[^cite_note-SlackChattopadhyay2009-442][^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] Ottawa is governed by the 25-member [Ottawa City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_City_Council) consisting of 24 councillors each representing one [ward.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ward_\(country_subdivision\))[^cite_note-445] The 25th member, the mayor, [Mark Sutcliffe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Sutcliffe) as of the [2022 Ottawa municipal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Ottawa_municipal_election), is elected in a citywide vote.[^cite_note-446] Ottawa is subject to Ontario's [strong mayor powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strong_Mayors,_Building_Homes_Act).[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448]

Along with being the capital of Canada, Ottawa is politically diverse in local politics. Most of the city has traditionally supported the [Liberal Party](149536.md) in federal elections.[^cite_note-Hill2002-449][^cite_note-450] The safest areas for the Liberals are the ones dominated by [Francophones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francophones), especially in Vanier and central Gloucester.[^cite_note-Hill2002-449] Central Ottawa is usually more [left](18499.md)-leaning, and the [New Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Democratic_Party_\(Canada\)) have won ridings there.[^cite_note-451] Ridings further outside the city centre, such as those including Kanata, Barrhaven and rural areas, tend to be more conservative, fiscally and socially.[^cite_note-Hill2002-449] This is especially true in the former Townships of [West Carleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Carleton_Township,_Ontario), [Goulbourn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goulbourn_Township,_Ontario), [Rideau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rideau_Township,_Ontario) and [Osgoode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osgoode_Township,_Ontario), which are more in line with the conservative areas in the surrounding [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County).[^cite_note-Hill2002-449]

At present, Ottawa is host to approximately 150 [embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Canada).[^cite_note-452] A further approximately 50 countries accredit their [embassies and missions in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Canada#Accredited_Embassies_and_High_Commissions) to Canada.[^cite_note-453]

## Transportation

See also: [OC Transpo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OC_Transpo), [O-Train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O-Train), [List of airports in the Ottawa area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_the_Ottawa_area), [List of Ottawa roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ottawa_roads), [List of numbered roads in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_numbered_roads_in_Ottawa), and [List of bridges in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_bridges_in_Ottawa)

### Public transportation

Main article: [OC Transpo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OC_Transpo)



An [O-Train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O-Train) crossing the [Rideau River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rideau_River). The O-Train is a [light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail) [public transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transportation) service provided by [OC Transpo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OC_Transpo).



Map of Ottawa's rapid transit network.

Ottawa's public transit system is managed by [OC Transpo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OC_Transpo).[^cite_note-oct-454] OC Transpo operates an integrated, multi-modal rapid transit system which includes:

- The [O-Train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O-Train) light rail system. The four-line public rail system includes three existing lines and one currently under construction.&#x20;

  - [Line 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_1_\(O-Train\)) is an east–west line which operates medium-capacity light rail vehicles and travels under the city's downtown core.

    [^cite_note-455]

  - [Line 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_2_\(O-Train\)) is a north–south rail transit corridor which utilizes a mix of Stadler FLIRTs and Alstom Coradia LINTs connecting the south end of Ottawa to Line 1 at [Bayview station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayview_station_\(Ottawa\)).

    [^cite_note-456]

    [^cite_note-457]

  - [Line 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_3_\(O-Train\)) is an under construction branch of Line 1, splitting at [Lincoln Fields station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Fields_station) and continuing west.

    [^cite_note-458]

  - [Line 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_4_\(O-Train\)) is a 4 km (2.5 mi) airport link connecting Line 2 to the [Ottawa Macdonald–Cartier International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Macdonald%E2%80%93Cartier_International_Airport)

    [^cite_note-459]

- A [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit) (BRT) system that uses a series of dedicated bus-only roadways named the [Transitway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transitway_\(Ottawa\)) and reserved lanes on city streets and highways. The Transitway has long distances between stops and substantial station amenities (including platforms, walkways, fare gates, ticket booths, elevators and convenience stores). It connects Ottawa's suburbs to the inner city.

  [^cite_note-460]

  &#x20;The rapid bus service network operates all day, seven days a week, reaching the suburban communities of Kanata to the West, Barrhaven to the South-West, Orléans to the East, and South Keys to the South.

  [^cite_note-\:1-461]

  &#x20;Sections of the transitway have been replaced by O-Train services.

  [^cite_note-462]

- Over 190 local bus routes are served by a fleet of ordinary, articulated and double-decker buses.

  [^cite_note-\:1-461]

  [^cite_note-463]

  [^cite_note-464]

  &#x20;Both OC Transpo and the Quebec-based [Société de transport de l'Outaouais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_de_transport_de_l'Outaouais) (STO) operate bus transit services between Ottawa and Gatineau.

  [^cite_note-465]

  [^cite_note-466]

  &#x20;OC Transpo also operates a door-to-door bus service for disabled individuals known as ParaTranspo.

  [^cite_note-oct-454]

  &#x20;There is a [hypothetical tramway partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatineau_tramway) that proposes linking Ottawa with Gatineau and connecting to the O-Train.

  [^cite_note-467]

### Airports

The [Ottawa Macdonald–Cartier International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Macdonald%E2%80%93Cartier_International_Airport) is the city's principal airport.[^cite_note-468] There are also three other airports providing general aviation services: [Gatineau-Ottawa Executive Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatineau-Ottawa_Executive_Airport), [Ottawa/Carp Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carp_Airport), and [Ottawa/Rockcliffe Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa/Rockcliffe_Airport).[^cite_note-PurdomCarroll2003f-469][^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471]

### Inter-city transportation

[Ottawa station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_station) is the [main](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_station) [inter-city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-city_rail) train station operated by [Via Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Via_Rail). It is located 4 kilometres (2.5 mi) to the east of [downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Ottawa) in [Eastway Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastway_Gardens) (adjacent to [O-Train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O-Train) [Tremblay station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tremblay_station)) and serves Via Rail's [Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qu%C3%A9bec_City%E2%80%93Windsor_Corridor_\(Via_Rail\)) Route.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] The city is also served by inter-city passenger rail service at [Fallowfield station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fallowfield_station_\(Ontario\)) in the southwestern suburban community of [Barrhaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrhaven).[^cite_note-475]

[Intercity bus services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercity_bus_service) are currently provided by several carriers at various stops throughout the city, following the closure of the former [Ottawa Central Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Central_Station) [bus terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_station) on 1 June 2021.[^cite_note-476] Major carriers include: [Megabus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megabus_\(North_America\)), [Ontario Northland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Northland_Motor_Coach_Services), Autobus Gatineau,[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] and [Orléans Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orl%C3%A9ans_Express).[^cite_note-480]

### Streets and highways

The City of Ottawa has over 8,400 km (5,200 mi) lane-kilometres of road and a series of freeways.[^cite_note-481] The primary freeways are the east–west provincial [Highway 417](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Highway_417) (designated as the Queensway and part of the [Trans-Canada Highway](70775.md)), Ottawa-Carleton [Highway 174](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Road_174) (formerly Provincial Highway 17), [Highway 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Highway_7), and the north–south provincial [Highway 416](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Highway_416) (designated as Veterans' Memorial Highway), which connects to other [400-Series highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/400-series_highways_\(Ontario\)) via the 401.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483][^cite_note-\:16-484] From downtown there are also connections to [Autoroute 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Autoroute_5) and [Autoroute 50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Autoroute_50), in neighbouring Gatineau.[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-\:16-484]

The city also has several scenic parkways and promenades, such as the [Kichi Zibi Mikan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kichi_Zibi_Mikan) (formerly the Macdonald Parkway), [Colonel By Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonel_By_Drive), [Queen Elizabeth Driveway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_Driveway), the [Sir George-Étienne Cartier Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_George-%C3%89tienne_Cartier_Parkway) (formerly the Rockcliffe Parkway), and the [Aviation Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviation_Parkway_\(Ottawa\)).[^cite_note-486] The National Capital Commission manages [ceremonial routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scenic_route) linking key attractions on both sides of the Ottawa River, including [Confederation Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederation_Boulevard).[^cite_note-487]

### Cycling and pedestrian network



The [Capital Pathway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Pathway) is a multi-use [trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail) interlinking sites throughout the [National Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_\(Canada\)).

Numerous paved [multi-use trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-use_trail), mostly operated by the [National Capital Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Commission) and the city, wind their way through much of the capital, including along the Ottawa River, Rideau River, and Rideau Canal.[^cite_note-488] [These pathways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Pathway) are used for transportation, tourism, and recreation. Because many streets either have wide curb lanes or bicycle lanes, cycling is a mode of transportation used by up to 3.7% of citizens, including in winter. This is the largest percentage of any major Canadian city.[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] As of 31 December 2015, over 900 km (560 mi) of cycling facilities are found in Ottawa, including 435 km (270 mi) of multi-use pathways, 8 km (5.0 mi) of cycle tracks, 200 km (120 mi) of on-road bicycle lanes, and 257 km (160 mi) of paved shoulders.[^cite_note-\:0-493] 204 km (127 mi) of new cycling facilities were added between 2011 and 2014.[^cite_note-\:0-493]

The entire length of [Sparks Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparks_Street) was turned into a [pedestrian mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedestrian_zone) in 1966.[^cite_note-494] Since 1960, additional avenues, streets, and parkways, are reserved for pedestrian and bicycle use only on Saturdays, Sundays and on selected holidays and events.[^cite_note-495] In 2021 city council unanimously approved the [Byward Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byward_Market) Public Realm Plan to make the market area more [car-free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carfree_city) and pedestrian friendly.[^cite_note-Public_Realm_Plan-496] From 2009 to 2015 the NCC introduced the [Capital Bixi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Bixi) [bicycle-sharing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle-sharing_system).[^cite_note-\:30-497] This continued until the company VeloGo took over the program from 2015 to 2018 when the partnership ceased.[^cite_note-498][^cite_note-\:30-497] [Scooter-sharing systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scooter-sharing_system) were introduced in the downtown and inner-city areas in 2025.[^cite_note-499]
